## Needs a decision

These are questions, not defects. A ruling moves to `DECISIONS.md` and closes the
row here. **This table is the questions with an id; most open questions are argued
inside an IDEAS paper's `## Open questions` and stay there.** `fli decisions` reads
both as one queue, and a question becomes pickable — by `fli decide` or on `fli gui`
— once its bullet carries lettered options and a `**Recommend X**` line. A row's
cell cannot hold options, so they go in the paper its Detail links.

| Id | Area | Question | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| <a id="fjs-d512"></a>FJS-D512 | litestone | **What does FJS-D143's zone binding spell for a wall-clock column whose zone lives on another model, and is the zone column's refusal a new @zone validator?** | Found fixing FJS-1214 · [IDEAS/time-and-recurrence.md](IDEAS/time-and-recurrence.md) |

| <a id="fjs-d371"></a>FJS-D371 | mesa | **Does VISION RULE 51 stand — must derived state live in a component?** It says reactive logic does not belong in a `.js` module. A `const` in `<script module>` is plain module-scope JavaScript, computed once (measured 2026-09-23), so a derivation that belongs to the domain has no reactive home outside an instance script, including in the Resource file the layout assigns to the domain. | [`IDEAS/review-solid-octane.md`](IDEAS/review-solid-octane.md) § Open questions |
| <a id="fjs-d366"></a>FJS-D366 | litestone · toolbelt · sierra | **Is *ordered within a scope, stable under concurrent insert* a column trait (`rank String @rank(scope: stateId)`), or an app pattern with a toolbelt helper?** Driven in linear (Phase 4), 50 concurrent drags into one gap. A fractional key is one write, one broadcast and no conflict per drag. An integer renumbered by the client is 1,625 requests, 1,568 × 409 and 40 shared positions. A server renumber either refuses 49 of 50 under `@version` or writes 2,850 rows and announces 50 ([`FJS-1308`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1308)). Offline, the fractional drag is 1 queued entry and the integer one is 57, of which 55 are refused. What the app still had to do itself: mint the key (the toolbelt has no helper), default it (`@default("a0")` put 46 issues on one key), break ties (a midpoint ties 50 of 50; jitter only helps if EVERY writer jitters), and bound it (≈300 drops at one spot pass 64 characters). | [`IDEAS/manual-order.md`](IDEAS/manual-order.md) § Open questions Q1 |

| <a id="fjs-711"></a>FJS-711 | caravan — **Caravan is correct under concurrency and unbounded everywhere a value is not a row: the cron grammar, the retry ladder, the payload and the missing operator verbs.** Umbrella for the remaining caravan findings. **`caravan-9`** — split out and **closed as [`FJS-767`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-767)**: the grammar is `@frontierjs/toolbelt/cron` now, one owner for two parsers, and everything above is refused at registration naming the field and the bound. The suggested remedy — *a boot warning where `nextRuns()` is null* — is superseded rather than done: with the sets bounded and an impossible day of month refused at parse, there is no expression left that registers and cannot fire. **`caravan-11`** — split out and **closed as [`FJS-1384`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1384)**: the ladder is jittered upward now. As found: the retry ladder is fixed, un-jittered and three attempts, plateaus at the last delay, and `retry()` resets `attempts = 0` — three jobs failing at one instant retried at gaps of `[60000, 60000, 60000]`, so 500 jobs against a downed provider all return at T+60 s exactly, which is the thundering herd a queue is bought to prevent; conduit has `backoffWithJitter` one package over. **`caravan-12`** — ruled [`FJS-D198`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d198): **Queue** is the noun, the set stays derived from `queueConf`, and the missing verbs are OPERATOR verbs — gated at ADMINISTRATOR or above, audited, and in no service file — where `dispatch`/`handle`/`schedule` are app verbs. A paused queue must be distinguishable from an idle one in `stats()`, and `queue('typo')` must refuse by name rather than `ensureQueue` creating it. Present and good are priority, delay, per-queue concurrency, dedup, cancel-pending, manual retry, cron and retention; absent are a per-queue RATE LIMIT (the commonest reason a queue exists, with no correct workaround per replica), cancelling a RUNNING job (`timeout` is advisory and `ctx` carries no `AbortSignal`), purge (**pause, resume and drain were built 2026-09-12** — `app.jobs.queue(name)`, a `queue_pauses` row the claim statement itself reads, ADMINISTRATOR over HTTP, `pausedMs` in `stats()`, `queue('typo')` refusing by name, and `fli check`'s `queue-operator-verb`; an incident can stop a queue without killing the process now, and purge is the verb of the four still missing), bulk dispatch (1000 jobs is 1000 fsyncs), and progress reporting. **`caravan-13`** — ruled [`FJS-D480`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d480), split out and **closed as [`FJS-1514`](#fjs-1514)**: dispatch refuses what JSON would not hand back, by job and key path, and a payload over 256 KB; the ruling's type half (`defineJob<T>` requiring a JSON-plain `T`) is not built, because a recursive JSON type refuses every `interface` payload. **`caravan-14`**: a job whose recorded actor has been deleted burns the whole retry ladder on a permanent condition and dies with an error about a principal rather than about the work, with the handler running zero times — a staff member leaving, a reseeded fixture or a deleted tenant, where the receipt is still owed; ruled [`FJS-D202`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d202): `onMissingActor` declared beside `maxAttempts`, **defaulting to `'fail'`** — fast and terminal, no ladder, the error naming the job and the actor id — because `'system'` as a default completes a departed staff member's work at level 8, above every gate the person passed. `'system'` stays available per job. **Split out and closed as [`FJS-1383`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1383)**: `onMissingActor` built 2026-09-26. Cites `FJS-D193`: a delivery needs a nameable principal. Promote any of these to its own id when it is worked | 2026-09-29 | An umbrella, not one defect: caravan-9, -11, -13 and -14 were each split out and closed (FJS-767, FJS-1384, FJS-1514, FJS-1383), and pause/resume/drain were built under FJS-D198. A re-probe of packages/caravan/src on 2026-09-28 found none of the five remaining gaps built, so each now has its own row: rate limit FJS-1522, cancelling a running job FJS-1523, purge FJS-1524, bulk dispatch FJS-1525, progress FJS-1526. · [cron.ts](packages/caravan/src/cron.ts) · [worker.ts](packages/caravan/src/worker.ts) · [caravan index.ts](packages/caravan/src/index.ts) · [types.ts](packages/caravan/src/types.ts) · [FJS-327](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-327) · [audit § 3.6](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/afba85f4-efe9-4959-92ca-a1b010dd2a16) |

| <a id="fjs-706"></a>FJS-706 | junction — **The service pipeline's construction-time surfaces return an object where they should return a verdict, so ten authoring mistakes compile green.** Umbrella for the medium and low core findings. **What is left, 2026-09-13, is three**: `core-8`, `core-12`, and `core-13`'s `health` shadow — every other sub-finding below is closed or ruled. Each is `D1`'s shape — fail-closed on the caller, fail-open on the author. **Ruled [`FJS-D199`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d199)**: an unknown AUTHORING key is refused by name, with every one of them reported at `start()` rather than thrown one boot at a time; what a CALLER sends stays tolerant, and there is no permitting flag. **`core-4`** — promoted to [`FJS-942`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-942) and closed. **`core-5`** — the two silences are [`FJS-945`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-945) and closed; the `around` half is [`FJS-946`](#fjs-946), which is a change to the shipped hook contract rather than a silence. **`core-6`** — promoted to [`FJS-939`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-939) and closed for the SECTION half; the nested half is [`FJS-940`](#fjs-940). **`core-7`** — the two lifecycle defects are [`FJS-950`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-950) and closed, and restart is [`FJS-949`](#fjs-949); the `ready()` half is not a defect — a throw in `ready` being logged while already serving is what this package's `CLAUDE.md` documents and `boot()` is the phase for anything that must succeed. **`core-8`**: measured, 20 concurrent creates with a 50 ms after-hook cost 60 ms with `transactional: false` and **1023 ms** with it on, fully serialized on SQLite's write lock — wants a bounded `transactional: { timeout }`. **`core-9`** — the one defect in it is [`FJS-951`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-951) and closed; the other three claims are measured DELIBERATE, with the rationale already written into the code, and the sub-finding's framing was wrong about them. **`core-10`** — **closed 2026-09-04, and the filing overstated it.** The guard IS structural and a row carrying `kind`/`object`/`data` did match; the `kind: 'list'` variant is the damaging one, because a list envelope is kept whole, so `protect()` stripped inside the row's own `data` column and the row's protected siblings went out intact. **The trigger was then measured rather than argued**: across every `.lite` here — this repo's apps, the seven imported real-world corpora and the 188-model `openmrp` fixture — no model carries even TWO of the three columns together, `object` occurs once in total, and `kind` is an enum nearly everywhere, so it holds `percent` and never `'single'`. **The branch it exploits also has no producer**: nothing outside `core/envelope.ts` imports `single()`/`list()`, and every legitimate pre-wrapped path (cache hit, idempotency replay, a `before` hook answering the call) sets `ctx.result` and skips the method, so it never reaches `raw` — disabling the passthrough left the suite at 1985 pass, 0 fail. So no Symbol brand: `errors` is a fourth discriminant, one line, and the brand is the answer if a real schema ever hits it. **Deleting the passthrough was the other candidate and is worse** — an app that did return an envelope from a custom method would have it double-wrapped, nested inside `data`, in silence. The tripwire carries the exact colliding row in both `kind` variants, with the same row plus `errors` beside it as the control. **`core-11`** — the ambient half is ruled `FJS-D198`'s neighbour [`FJS-D200`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d200): `$` is a CLOSED set the framework owns, so a timeout and an `AbortSignal` may land there and app state may not. No per-call timeout, no `AbortSignal` into a call, no trace parent (a nested call is a sibling), and `app.service('x')` is `Promise<unknown>` on the server while `ServiceTypes` types the browser. **`core-12`**: `registerErrorMapper` and `setServiceCache` are process-global across apps, `app.claim()` is bypassable by plain assignment, and `ready()` runs after `listen`. **`core-13`** — the loader's three silences are [`FJS-944`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-944) and closed; the `health` shadow is a ROUTING question with a different owner and stays here, unworked. Promote any of these to its own id when it is worked | 2026-09-29 | Umbrella split out, nothing left under it. core-8 is ruled FJS-D471: no bounded-wait option, and slow after-hook work goes through ctx.enqueue. core-12 is promoted to FJS-1510 and the health shadow (core-13) to FJS-1511, as the row asked once they were worked. Every other sub-finding was already closed or ruled under its own id. · [service.ts](packages/junction/src/core/service.ts) · [app.ts](packages/junction/src/core/app.ts) · [loader.ts](packages/junction/src/core/loader.ts) · [config](packages/junction/src/config/index.ts) · [envelope.ts](packages/junction/src/core/envelope.ts) · [audit § 3.1](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/afba85f4-efe9-4959-92ca-a1b010dd2a16) |

| <a id="fjs-1493"></a>FJS-1493 | mesa · sierra — **`layout:` in a `.md` file's frontmatter was parsed into `ctx.layout` and read by nothing, so a Markdown file could not name the component that wraps it — which is how every ksite block is written.** | 2026-09-28 | Found by the *ksite stressor*, Phase 1. **Mesa wraps and Sierra names.** `compileMd` takes `layouts` (name → file) and wraps the body in the named component. The layout receives every frontmatter key as a prop, which the parent may override, plus the parent's `class` and `$attributes`, and it gets the body both as element children and as the `children` prop. A name the map does not hold is a compile error listing the names it does, and `layout: false` or no map wraps nothing. The wrap happens after Markdown because CommonMark cannot carry a tag line with `{...$attributes}` on it. Sierra's `markdownLayouts: [dirs]` builds the map: every `.mesa`/`.md` file directly in each directory, named by basename, with a later directory winning a name (ksite's mdsvex map had the same order). Layouts are a namespace apart from `autoImport`, as ksite's were, so `Trust.md` can say `layout: Trust`. The map reaches the Vite transform and the prerender alike. [md-layout.test.js](packages/mesa/test/md-layout.test.js) · [markdown-layouts.test.js](packages/sierra/test/markdown-layouts.test.js) · [static-autoimport-build.test.js](packages/sierra/test/static-autoimport-build.test.js). Held by [static-autoimport-build.test.js](packages/sierra/test/static-autoimport-build.test.js), a real static build of a Markdown page that imports nothing, which fails with the original message when the hook is removed. Also by `render-component.test.js` § options.transformSource, whose negative control is the untransformed tree. `example`: `build:site` prerenders 18 pages and `verify:site` passes 45/45. |
| <a id="fjs-1492"></a>FJS-1492 | sierra · mesa — **A `.md` component's frontmatter keys were props in the build and undeclared in dev: Sierra stripped the `---` block before `compileMd` could turn it into exports.** | 2026-09-28 | Fixed with [FJS-1491](#fjs-1491), by keeping them in both. `prepareForCompile` hands a `.md` file back with its frontmatter block in front and the injected `<script>` directly after it, where `compileMd` reads each. It also leaves the file's fences to `compileMd`. A `.mesa` route's frontmatter is still build metadata and still stripped. The ksite port's dev transform of `Hero.md` now declares `layout`, `template` and `classes`. [prepare-for-compile.test.js](packages/sierra/test/prepare-for-compile.test.js). |
| <a id="fjs-1499"></a>FJS-1499 | sierra — **`injectAutoImports` read tags inside HTML comments and could import a file into itself: `Image.mesa`, whose doc comment shows `<Image source="…" />`, imported `Image` and died on *"Image" has already been declared*, in dev and in the build.** | 2026-09-28 | Found by the *ksite stressor*, Phase 1, porting ksite's `<Image />`. The tag scan now strips `<!-- … -->` along with `<script>`, and `injectAutoImports(source, map, file)` skips any entry that is the file being compiled, because Mesa names the component function after its file. `test/auto-import.test.js` § comments and the file itself, including the negative control (the same tag, uncommented, in another file, is still imported). |
| <a id="fjs-1483"></a>FJS-1483 | junction — **Over the socket, the browser client's Idempotency-Key is never claimed: the client sends it in the frame's meta.headers and the server's enterRequest reads only meta.idempotencyKey, so a held write replayed over a live socket runs twice** | 2026-09-29 | The socket path handed enterRequest only meta.idempotencyKey, a spelling nothing sends, while the browser client puts the key in meta.headers['idempotency-key'] beside its other per-call headers; so claimIdempotency never saw it. channels.ts now passes the frame's own protocol headers (lower-cased, limited to PROTOCOL_CALL_HEADERS, never the upgrade's, which would make every later write a replay) to enterRequest as headers, the one reader of the key and the made-at instants on both transports; meta.idempotencyKey is dropped. Pinned by test/idempotency.test.ts § over the socket — three authenticated frames sharing a key ran the create 3 times before, once now. · [channels.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/channels.ts) · [client _call](packages/junction/src/client/index.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1278"></a>FJS-1278 | sierra · junction · litestone — **A replayed write reaches the boundary with no statement of WHEN it was made, so a server that stamps `now()` dates a whole shift's work at the moment the network came back.** **Wants a ruling, not only a fix.** | 2026-09-29 | Built to FJS-D469. A held write now states when it was made: sierra's pending and attachment drains pass the entry's createdAt as madeAt, and the junction client sends it as X-Fjs-Made-At beside X-Fjs-Sent-At, which it reads off its own clock at the send. They go as headers over HTTP and in the frame's meta.headers over the socket. enterRequest is the one reader: resolveMadeAt moves the made-at by the device's offset (the server's now minus sentAt), so a clock that is wrong by a constant amount is corrected exactly. It refuses a made-at with no sent-at, one later than its sent-at, and an instant that does not parse, each with a 400. callService sets ctx.madeAt on every call, which is the arrival on a live call. installMadeAt hands litestone a closure over the request store through the new root db.$madeAt(fn), and @default(now()) and @updatedAt stamp from it. Predicates, @@expires and the audit trail stay on the landing time. Proved by junction test/made-at.test.ts (8 h back with a 3 h device skew, both transports, the refusals, the client headers), test/context-contract.test.ts § madeAt, litestone test/made-at.test.ts and sierra test/pending-queue.test.js. The sierra test goes red with the one line removed. All three suites are green and junction typechecks clean. fli prove: 15/28. The 13 failures are tagged open, or fail the same way in a HEAD worktree carrying the other sessions' edits (example verify, verify:build budget 923/899 kB, basecamp verify and verify:outpost), or are db state (verify:stock, which fails at baseline too given the same db). · [verify-duration.mjs](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/web/test/verify-duration.mjs) · [`FJS-1214`](#fjs-1214) · [pending.js `_send`](packages/sierra/src/junction/pending.js) · [FJS-D298](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d298) · [FJS-D300](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d300) |
| <a id="fjs-1309"></a>FJS-1309 | sierra — **A resource remembers only the last 200 rows it read, and a patch of a forgotten row goes up with no version, so on any `@version` model a screen that shows more than 200 rows has every edit to its first rows refused `400 Issue.version is @version — an update must carry the version it read`.** | 2026-09-29 | The read-version map was the last 200 rows in insertion order, and a pushed row was never read, so a patch of either went up with no version and was refused 400 in the server's words, or at the offline drain. Built to FJS-D468: _remember evicts only rows whose node no view holds, the first sight of a row in a held view (the resource store's subscription, and record() over a node already held) is remembered as its read and never replaces one, and a @version patch that still has no version is refused on the device with code VERSION_UNREAD and a reload-it message before anything is sent or queued. Proved by packages/sierra/test/resource-read-versions.test.js (8, red before the fix) and the sierra suite (1800); example verify:revisions passed every @version edit check in the browser before stopping at FJS-1382. · [verify-order-offline.mjs](../fjs-prototypes/linear/web/test/verify-order-offline.mjs) · [`FJS-1302`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1302) · [`FJS-823`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-823) · [resource.js `READ_CACHE_MAX`, `_remember`, `_readVersion`](packages/sierra/src/junction/resource.js) · [verify-screens.mjs](../fjs-prototypes/linear/web/test/verify-screens.mjs) |
| <a id="fjs-1316"></a>FJS-1316 | junction — **Under `strategy row`, a claim that takes a database read can reach a broadcast only as a snapshot taken when the socket opened — so a person removed from a private team keeps receiving its rows live, on the socket they already have, until they reconnect.** | 2026-09-29 | The resolver was synchronous, so a claim that needs a database read (linear's teamIds, memberRole) could only be read when the socket opened and was stale for as long as it stayed open. A Promise it returned was an object with no keys and was silently read as no claim. Under FJS-D472 A, ChannelClaimsFn may return a Promise, and gradeRecipients buckets connections by (principal, channel), awaits the resolver once per bucket on every frame (the buckets in parallel) and caches nothing, as the request path does. A rejection refuses that bucket alone and warns. Pinned by test/channel-claims.test.ts § a resolver that reads the database: a member removed mid-session stops receiving, one added starts, the resolver is called once per principal per channel per frame across three tabs, and a rejecting resolver spares the other recipients. All four were red before the fix, and junction's 2475 tests pass. The linear prototype still reads its claims in the 'connection' handler and has to move them into the resolver to benefit. fli prove's drives did not run: port 8110 is held by another process, and basecamp's verify needs a database reset that another live session would lose. · [`FJS-D191`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d191) · [`FJS-1291`](#fjs-1291) · [linear `core/channels.ts`](../fjs-prototypes/linear/api/src/core/channels.ts) · [channels.ts `ChannelClaimsFn`](packages/junction/src/transport/channels.ts) · [FJS-749](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-749) |
| <a id="fjs-d509"></a>FJS-D509 | junction — **Should the transport gzip a raw route's own stream Response, which it cannot tell from a string body?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D509`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d509): **A** — Keep the contract: a streamed body is built with `ctx.stream()` or names `content-encoding`. Put that in the raw-route docs and the api-hazards entry, and have dev mode warn when an `arrayBuffer()` for compression waits longer than N ms. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d510"></a>FJS-D510 | orion — **Where does the flow canvas live: a graph component in @frontierjs/ui, or a canvas local to orion's web surface?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D510`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d510): **A** — an orion-local canvas under `packages/orion/web/`, reading `FlowLayout` directly; one consumer, no kit API to design up front. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d513"></a>FJS-D513 | toolbelt — **Does the shared predicate language (FJS-D271) grow a value-assembling form (template literal, object literal), or do orion's assembling shapes stay documents?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D513`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d513): **A** — Grow `template` and `object` in the shared parser; litestone's policy compiler refuses both by name at parse of a `.lite` policy (a value-assembling form is not a predicate). · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d508"></a>FJS-D508 | cli — **Where does an app declare its fli check allowances ('<rule>:<path>' → why)?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D508`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d508): **A** — An `allow` object inside `check-baseline.json`, beside the counts. One file holds an app's check policy; `readBaseline` already skips non-numeric keys, so it reads the object alongside. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1437"></a>FJS-1437 | junction — **A custom method's `gate:` is a level, so a method whose work touches no row cannot say *a caller holding this claim*. *Portal, the search stressor*'s Privacy Pass search opens with `gate: 0`, which admits every stranger, and is shut again only because its first act is a write the schema grades. Drop that line and strangers search for nothing, with no declaration to object** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled FJS-D514 A. methods: [{ method, gate: 0, claims: ['passId'] }] -- gateAuthAround grades every named claim present and non-null on what the resolver merged (guest 401, session 403, before the body); refused on a CRUD verb; svc.describe().methodClaims reports it. Pinned: junction test/method-claims.test.ts over HTTP. Portal's search still spells gate: 0 and should move to claims:. · [litestone.ts:2857](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) · [FJS-1412](#fjs-1412) · [FJS-1414](#fjs-1414) · [FJS-1413](#fjs-1413) · [portal api/test/passes.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/test/passes.test.ts) · [portal db/schema.lite § Privacy Pass](../fjs-prototypes/portal/db/schema.lite) · [portal core/pass-claim.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/core/pass-claim.ts) · [portal fakes/drive-pass.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/fakes/drive-pass.ts) · [IDEAS/bearer-access.md](IDEAS/bearer-access.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1462"></a>FJS-1462 | litestone · junction · basecamp — **A claims-only guest still grades above STRANGER wherever `getLevel` is not the scaffold's: Litestone's default `gradeStanding` answers CREATOR(3) for `{ ...claims }`, and basecamp's and the examples' hand-written `getLevel`s answer USER** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled FJS-D515 A. toolbelt isIdentified(): no id and no userId grades STRANGER in gradeStanding, and GatePlugin never asks an app getLevel about such a principal, so basecamp's and the example's hand-written getLevels are no longer reached for a guest. Pinned: toolbelt gate.spec 'no identity is a stranger', litestone elegance-fixes 'a resolver is never asked about a principal with no identity'. · [gate.js](packages/toolbelt/src/gate/gate.js) · [basecamp gate.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/core/gate.ts) · [litestone.ts `mergeClaims`](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-d520"></a>FJS-D520 | auth — **How does a row-tenanted app give a registering user their tenant — an onRegistered hook in the create transaction, createUser passing schema-declared User columns, or an app-side recipe?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D520`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d520): **A** — `onRegistered({ user, db })`, run inside the same transaction as the user insert; it may create the Account and membership and write the tenant column, and a throw rolls back the whole registration. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d518"></a>FJS-D518 | litestone — **Where does a data migration live: outside migrations/ (docs name seed/an app script), or a .js migration that declares rows-only and the shadow skips?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D518`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d518): **A** — A data change is not a migration. `migrations/` holds `.sql` only, and the docs name the home for a data change: an app script chained after `migrate apply` and made idempotent by the rows it expects, which is what remnant chose (`remnant/PLAN.md` Q2). `.js` in the history becomes a refusal with a nonzero exit. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d506"></a>FJS-D506 | litestone — **Should a numeric validator (@gte, @lte, @gt, @lt) also emit a column CHECK, so an atomic operator on that column is allowed rather than refused?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D506`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d506): **A** — Emit `CHECK (col >= n)` from each numeric validator, the way `@scale`/`@money` already emit theirs, and let the operator through. A migration, a seed, a raw statement and `asSystem()` are then held as well. The cost is a rebuild migration for every existing schema that carries one, and a CHECK violation needs the validator's message rather than SQLite's. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d521"></a>FJS-D521 | litestone — **Should a refused foreign key throw a new ForeignKeyError, or should one constraint error class carry model/field/value for unique, FK and datatype alike?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D521`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d521): **A** — `ForeignKeyError` beside `UniqueConflictError` in `core/errors.js`, built in `client.js` by checking the refused row's declared relations; datatype becomes a `ValidationError` before the write. junction gives it `status` 422. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d522"></a>FJS-D522 | junction — **How does an app compose two principal claim sources so each stays described in the principal snapshot?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D522`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d522): **A** — `principal` also takes an array: resolvers run in order, results merged, a claim name emitted by two refused, the snapshot describes each element (a plain function still reads "describes nothing", but only for itself). · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d515"></a>FJS-D515 | litestone — **Who grades a claims-only guest STRANGER — Litestone's gate for any principal without an id, a guest marker mergeClaims sets, or each app's getLevel?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D515`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d515): **A** — Litestone owns it: `GatePlugin` grades a principal with neither `id` nor `userId` STRANGER before any `getLevel` runs, and `gradeStanding` makes the same test so Junction's `sessionGateLevel` and Caravan agree. After `toDataPrincipal`, every session already carries `id`, so no real session changes grade. The cost: a test or app calling `$setAuth({ role: 'admin' })` without an `id` now grades 0, which is three call sites in `litestone.test.ts` and any more that grep finds. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |

## See also

- `ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md` — closed rows aged out of § Closed. Same table, verbatim. Read when tracing a specific id, never cold.
- `CLAUDE.md` § Live hazards — traps that are **correct behavior**; knowing them saves a day. Not issues.
- `DECISIONS.md` — settled rulings. A `FJS-D##` closing here lands there.
- `IDEAS/` — roadmap and design records for work not started. `IDEAS/overview.md`'s wave 0 is the subset of this file that happens to be argued in an ideas file.
- `HANDOFF.md` — per-session narrative, newest first. History, not a ledger.
- `VERIFYING.md` — how to promote a `stale?` row to `open` or `closed`.
